Since I was moved into that prototype-testing role about a year and a month ago, it’s been pretty continuous. I never heard voices in the lab or felt like people were building a case against me until after I got really low after the role change. Like I said, my miniatures printer has just been sitting there with the same half-finished figure for months. And yoga or walking in the woods used to feel good, but now they just feel like another task I’m supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a clear change. I don’t weigh myself much, so I’m not totally sure, but my clothes seem about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I feel really slowed down. At work, people have started finishing my sentences in meetings because it takes me so long to answer. And even just walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over small mistakes I’ve made in the lab, even things that probably weren’t a big deal, and I can’t let them go. It turns into me thinking I’m not just bad at my job, but that I’m a bad person. Like if something happens because of it, I deserve it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Not really restless. It’s more the opposite—I feel slowed down. People at work have been finishing my sentences in meetings because I take so long to answer, and even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I’ve had thoughts like that. On my commute, I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t arrive anywhere. And sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and it scares me enough that I grip the wheel and pull over.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a year and a month now. It started after I got moved into the prototype-testing role.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: Yes. Mostly in the lab after hours. I’ll hear someone behind the test bench say, “You ruined it,” really clearly, like a coworker is standing there. But when I turn around, there’s no one there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Since I was moved into that prototype-testing role about a year and a month ago, it’s been pretty continuous. I can’t think of a couple months where I felt okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where the voices or unusual thoughts you mentioned were absent?\n\nPatient: Before this all started, no, I didn’t have anything like that. I never heard voices in the lab or felt like people were building a case against me until after I got really low after the role change. It seems to only happen when I’m in that guilty, depressed state.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where these feelings of sadness, guilt, and worthlessness were so overwhelming that you couldn't do anything at all?\n\nPatient: Yes. I get stuck replaying tiny mistakes from the lab over and over, and it feels like proof that I’m not just bad at the job, but bad as a person. Sometimes the guilt gets so heavy that I feel like I deserve whatever happens because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt so low that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: Not exactly unable to get out of bed, but I’ve been really slowed down. Like, at work people have started finishing my sentences in meetings because it takes me so long to answer. Even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where the voices or unusual thoughts stopped, but the feelings of sadness, guilt, and worthlessness remained?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. The voices and those thoughts about people building a case against me seem to come with the really low, guilty state. Before I got depressed after the role change, I never had anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or had unusual thoughts when you weren't feeling sad, guilty, or worthless?\n\nPatient: No. I don’t think I’ve ever had the voices in the lab or that feeling that people were building a case against me when I wasn’t already feeling really low and guilty. It’s been really hard to get through normal work tasks.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that. I know I haven’t felt like myself, but I don’t know if I’d call it sad exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: Not really. My miniature printer has had the same half-finished figure sitting there for months. And even things like yoga or going for a walk in the woods just feel like chores now, not something I actually want to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things that normally bring you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. Like I said, my miniatures printer has just been sitting there with the same half-finished figure for months. And yoga or walking in the woods used to feel good, but now they just feel like another task I’m supposed to do.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a clear change. I don’t weigh myself much, so I’m not totally sure, but my clothes seem about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I feel really slowed down. At work, people have started finishing my sentences in meetings because it takes me so long to answer. And even just walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over small mistakes I’ve made in the lab, even things that probably weren’t a big deal, and I can’t let them go. It turns into me thinking I’m not just bad at my job, but that I’m a bad person. Like if something happens because of it, I deserve it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Not really restless. It’s more the opposite—I feel slowed down. People at work have been finishing my sentences in meetings because I take so long to answer, and even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes… I’ve had thoughts like that. On my commute, I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t arrive anywhere. And sometimes the thought of steering off the road comes up, and it scares me enough that I grip the wheel and pull over.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a year and a month now. It started after I got moved into the prototype-testing role.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: Yes. Mostly in the lab after hours. I’ll hear someone behind the test bench say, “You ruined it,” really clearly, like a coworker is standing there. But when I turn around, there’s no one there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Since I was moved into that prototype-testing role about a year and a month ago, it’s been pretty continuous. I can’t think of a couple months where I felt okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where the voices or unusual thoughts you mentioned were absent?\n\nPatient: Before this all started, no, I didn’t have anything like that. I never heard voices in the lab or felt like people were building a case against me until after I got really low after the role change. It seems to only happen when I’m in that guilty, depressed state.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where these feelings of sadness, guilt, and worthlessness were so overwhelming that you couldn't do anything at all?\n\nPatient: Yes. I get stuck replaying tiny mistakes from the lab over and over, and it feels like proof that I’m not just bad at the job, but bad as a person. Sometimes the guilt gets so heavy that I feel like I deserve whatever happens because of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt so low that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: Not exactly unable to get out of bed, but I’ve been really slowed down. Like, at work people have started finishing my sentences in meetings because it takes me so long to answer. Even walking from my desk to the test chamber feels like I’m moving through thick syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where the voices or unusual thoughts stopped, but the feelings of sadness, guilt, and worthlessness remained?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. The voices and those thoughts about people building a case against me seem to come with the really low, guilty state. Before I got depressed after the role change, I never had anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or had unusual thoughts when you weren't feeling sad, guilty, or worthless?\n\nPatient: No. I don’t think I’ve ever had the voices in the lab or that feeling that people were building a case against me when I wasn’t already feeling really low and guilty. That all started after the role change, with the depression.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt so low that you couldn't leave the house?\n\nPatient: Yes. There have been times I didn’t really leave the house except when I absolutely had to. I mostly stopped seeing people and stopped doing the routines that used to keep me steady. Work has been affected too—I missed an important project deadline, and now coworkers have to double-check my reports or take over parts I used to handle myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

    "duration_verified": "symptoms present for over 1 year",
    "functional_impairment": true,
    "traumatic_stressor": null,
    "psychosocial_stressor": true,
    "additional_requirements": [
      "onset after role change",
      "persistent feelings of guilt and worthlessness",
      "psychomotor retardation"
    ]
  }
}
